Q1Biology / botanyno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchbotany
chapterPhotosynthesis in Higher Plants high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the light-reaction electron transport chain, where plastoquinone carries electrons from PS-II to the Cytb6f complex, taught in Photosynthesis in Higher Plants.
subtopic 1Light Reaction, Electron Transport and Splitting Of Water
why this subtopicThe answer depends on plastoquinone's role as a mobile electron carrier in the light-reaction electron transport chain between PS-II and the Cytb6f complex.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
In light reaction, plastoquinone facilitates the transfer of electrons from
  1. PS-I to NADP+
  2. PS-I to ATP synthase
  3. PS-II to Cytb6f complex
  4. Cytb6f complex to PS-I
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
  1. In the Z-scheme the electron flow is PS-II → PQ → Cyt b6f → PC → PS-I, so plastoquinone carries electrons from PS-II to the Cytb6f complex, which is option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—3 highunchecked
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall plastoquinone's position in the Z-scheme electron transport chain of the light reaction and match it to the option naming the carriers on either side of it
whyThe work is a single recall of where plastoquinone sits in the NCERT Z-scheme, with no application or calculation.
the catchOption D (Cyt b6f complex to PS-I) is the role of plastocyanin, the next mobile carrier, so a candidate who mixes up the two mobile carriers is drawn to it.

why this subtopicThe answer depends on the palindromic recognition sequence of the restriction endonuclease EcoRI, which is taught under restriction enzymes.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The specific palindromic sequence which is recognized by EcoRI is
  1. 5’ - CTTAAG - 3’
    3’ - GAATTC - 5’
  2. 5’ - GGATCC - 3’
    3’ - CCTAGG - 5’
  3. 5’ - GAATTC - 3’
    3’ - CTTAAG - 5’
  4. 5’ - GGAACC - 3’
    3’ - CCTTGG - 5’
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

  1. EcoRI recognizes the palindromic sequence 5'-GAATTC-3' paired with 3'-CTTAAG-5', which is option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—3 highunchecked

Q4Biology / zoologyno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterHuman Health and Disease high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the properties of active and passive immunity (active immunity is slow to develop), which Human Health and Disease teaches.
subtopic 1Active and Passive Immunity
why this subtopicEvery option, including the wrong one (active immunity is slow, not quick), turns on the distinction between active and passive immunity.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Identify the wrong statement with reference to immunity.
  1. Active immunity is quick and gives full response
  2. Foetus receives some antibodies from mother, it is an example for passive immunity
  3. When exposed to antigen (living or dead) antibodies are produced in the host’s body. It is called “Active immunity”
  4. When ready-made antibodies are directly given, it is called “Passive immunity”
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. Active immunity develops when the host's own body produces antibodies after exposure to a living or dead antigen; it is slow and takes time to give its full effective response, so option 1 is wrong.
  2. Ready-made antibodies given directly, including antibodies passed from mother to foetus through the placenta, give passive immunity, so options 2, 3 and 4 are correct.
  3. The wrong statement is option 1.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—1 highunchecked
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall the NCERT description of active versus passive immunity (active immunity is slow and takes time to give its full response; passive immunity uses ready-made antibodies, e.g. maternal antibodies to the foetus) and match each statement against it to spot the one that contradicts the text
whyThe work is a single recall of the NCERT paragraph on active and passive immunity, which the four statements paraphrase almost word for word.

Q6Biology / botanyno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchbotany
chapterMineral Nutrition high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the specific roles of essential mineral elements (Fe, Zn, Mn and the missing item (c)) in plants, which is taught in Mineral Nutrition.
subtopic 1Essential Mineral Elements: Macronutrients and Micronutrients
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the specific roles of micronutrients (iron, zinc, manganese and the missing item (c)) in plants, which is taught under essential mineral elements.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Match the following concerning essential elements and their functions in plants
(a)Iron(i)Photolysis of water
(b)Zinc(ii)Pollen germination
(d)Manganese(iii)Required for chlorophyll biosynthesis
(iv)IAA biosynthesis
  1. (a) - (iii); (b) - (iv); (c) - (ii); (d) - (i)
  2. (a) - (iv); (b) - (i); (c) - (ii); (d) - (iii)
  3. (a) - (ii); (b) - (i); (c) - (iv); (d) - (iii)
  4. (a) - (iv); (b) - (iii); (c) - (ii); (d) - (i)
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. Iron is needed for chlorophyll biosynthesis, so (a) → (iii).
  2. Zinc is needed for auxin (IAA) synthesis, so (b) → (iv).
  3. Manganese takes part in the splitting (photolysis) of water in photosynthesis, so (d) → (i).
  4. The remaining item (c) pairs with pollen germination (ii), a boron function.
  5. (a)-(iii); (b)-(iv); (c)-(ii); (d)-(i) is option 1.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—1 highunchecked
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall the function of iron in plants and place it against List II (chlorophyll biosynthesis vs IAA biosynthesis vs photolysis) → Recall the functions of zinc and manganese and match them (IAA biosynthesis; photolysis of water / oxygen evolution) → Use those three pairings to eliminate options, with the remaining element (c) left on pollen germination
whyThe work is three linked NCERT mineral-nutrition recalls (Fe, Zn, Mn functions) followed by option elimination; no calculation or cross-chapter insight is needed.
the catchOptions 1 and 4 share (c)-(ii) and (d)-(i) and differ only by swapping iron and zinc between chlorophyll and IAA biosynthesis, so a candidate who confuses Fe and Zn roles lands on the trap.

Q9Biology / zoologyno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterReproductive Health high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on which assisted reproductive technologies (ZIFT, IUT, GIFT, ICSI) involve embryo transfer to help infertile females, which Reproductive Health teaches under ART.
subtopic 1Assisted Reproductive Technologies (ART)
why this subtopicThe answer depends on knowing which assisted reproductive techniques transfer an embryo (ZIFT and IUT) rather than gametes (GIFT) or injecting sperm (ICSI).
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
In which of the following techniques, the embryo are transferred to assist those females who cannot conceive?
  1. ICSI and ZIFT
  2. GIFT and ICSI
  3. ZIFT and IUT
  4. GIFT and ZIFT
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
  1. In ZIFT the zygote or early embryo with up to 8 blastomeres is transferred into the fallopian tube, so it is an embryo transfer.
  2. In IUT an embryo with more than 8 blastomeres is transferred into the uterus, so it is also an embryo transfer.
  3. GIFT transfers an ovum (a gamete), not an embryo, and ICSI injects a sperm directly into the ovum in the laboratory, so neither is an embryo transfer.
  4. Only ZIFT and IUT are embryo-transfer techniques, which is option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—3 highunchecked
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall from the NCERT Reproductive Health list of assisted reproductive technologies what each technique transfers (ZIFT and IUT transfer the zygote or early embryo; GIFT transfers gametes; ICSI injects a sperm into the ovum), then pick the pair in which both transfer an embryo
whyThe work is one recall of the NCERT definitions of four ART techniques, with each option checked against what that technique transfers.

Q11Biology / zoologyno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterBreathing and Exchange of Gases high confidence
why this chapterEvery option turns on the factors (pO2, pCO2, H+ concentration) that favour oxyhaemoglobin formation in the alveoli, which is the transport of gases taught in Breathing and Exchange of Gases.
subtopic 1Transport of Gases
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the factors (pO2, pCO2, H+ concentration) that favour oxyhaemoglobin formation, which is taught under transport of oxygen in Transport of Gases.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Identify the wrong statement with reference to transport of oxygen.
  1. Higher H+ conc. In alveoli favours the formation of oxyhaemoglobin
  2. Low pCO2 in alveoli favours the formation of oxyhaemoglobin
  3. Binding of oxygen with haemoglobin is mainly related to partial pressure of O2
  4. Partial pressure of CO2 can interfere with O2 binding with haemoglobin
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. Conditions in the alveoli that favour oxyhaemoglobin formation are high pO2, low pCO2, lesser H+ concentration and lower temperature.
  2. Higher H+ concentration favours dissociation of oxygen from oxyhaemoglobin (as in tissues), so statement 1 is wrong.
  3. Statements 2, 3 and 4 are correct as stated, so the wrong statement is option 1.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—1 highunchecked
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall the NCERT list of alveolar conditions that favour oxyhaemoglobin formation (high pO2, low pCO2, lower H+ concentration, lower temperature) and spot the option that contradicts it
whyThe work is a single recall of the factors NCERT names as favouring O2 binding to haemoglobin in the alveoli, checked against four statements.

subjectBiology
branchboth

subtopic 1Animal Breeding

printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
By which method was a new breed ‘Hisardale’ of sheep formed by using Bikaneri ewes and Marino rams?
  1. Cross breeding
  2. Inbreeding
  3. Out crossing
  4. Mutational breeding
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. Mating superior males of one breed with superior females of a different breed, combining the desirable qualities of both, is cross-breeding; Hisardale was produced this way from Bikaneri ewes and Marino rams, so the answer is option 1.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—1 highunchecked
difficultyeasy · 1 step

whyThe work is one direct recall of a named example the NCERT animal-breeding section gives verbatim for cross-breeding.
the catchOption 'Out crossing' is a close neighbour that a candidate who remembers only 'mating unrelated animals' could pick, since out-crossing is defined as mating within the same breed without common ancestors; also, the Strategies for Enhancement in Food Production chapter was dropped from the rationalised current NCERT Class 12 book.

Q14Biology / zoologyno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterChemical Coordination and Integration high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on matching endocrine glands (pituitary, thyroid, adrenal, pancreas) to the hormonal disorders caused by their dysfunction, which is taught in Chemical Coordination and Integration.
subtopic 1Endocrine Disorders
why this subtopicThe answer depends on matching each gland to the disorder its hormone imbalance causes (pituitary-diabetes insipidus, thyroid-Graves' disease, adrenal-Addison's disease, pancreas-diabetes mellitus), which is the endocrine disorders topic.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Match the following columns and select the correct option.
aPituitary glandiGrave’s disease
bThyroid glandiiDiabetes mellitus
cAdrenal glandiiiDiabetes insipidus
dPancreasivAddison’s disease
  1. (a) - (iii); (b) - (i); (c) - (iv); (d) - (ii)
  2. (a) - (ii); (b) - (i); (c) - (iv); (d) - (iii)
  3. (a) - (iv); (b) - (iii); (c) - (i); (d) - (ii)
  4. (a) - (iii); (b) - (ii); (c) - (i); (d) - (iv)
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. Pituitary gland: deficient ADH from the posterior pituitary causes diabetes insipidus, so (a) → (iii).
  2. Thyroid gland: hyperthyroidism (exophthalmic goitre) is Graves' disease, so (b) → (i).
  3. Adrenal gland: underproduction of adrenal cortex hormones causes Addison's disease, so (c) → (iv).
  4. Pancreas: insulin deficiency from the islets causes diabetes mellitus, so (d) → (ii).
  5. The set (a)-(iii); (b)-(i); (c)-(iv); (d)-(ii) is option 1.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—1 highunchecked
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall the NCERT gland-disorder pairs (pituitary ADH deficiency, thyroid hyperthyroidism, adrenal cortex hypofunction, pancreatic insulin deficiency) and pick the option that matches them; two pairs already single out one option
whyThe work is one direct recall of four standard endocrine disorders from the Chemical Coordination chapter, matched against options that differ in obvious pairings.

Q16Biology / bothno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchboth
chapterBiotechnology: Principles and Processes high confidence
why this chapterThe question turns on the functions of nucleases, exonucleases, ligases and polymerases as tools of recombinant DNA technology (ligases join DNA molecules), taught in Biotechnology: Principles and Processes.
subtopic 1Tools of Recombinant DNA Technology
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the functions of the enzymes used as tools of rDNA technology (nucleases, ligases that join DNA molecules, polymerases), which is taught under Tools of Recombinant DNA Technology.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Choose the correct pair from the following:
  1. Nuclease - Separate the two strange of DNA
  2. Exonuclease - Make cuts at specific positions within DNA
  3. Ligases - Join the two DNA molecules
  4. Polymerases - Break the DNA into fragments
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
  1. Nucleases hydrolyse phosphodiester bonds; they do not separate the two strands of DNA, so option 1 is wrong.
  2. Exonucleases remove nucleotides from the ends of DNA; cutting at specific positions within DNA is done by endonucleases, so option 2 is wrong.
  3. DNA polymerases synthesise DNA; they do not break DNA into fragments, so option 4 is wrong.
  4. DNA ligases join DNA fragments/molecules together, so option 3 is the correct pair.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—3 highunchecked
difficultyeasy · 1 step
stepsRecall the functions of the enzymes listed in NCERT Biotechnology: Principles and Processes (nucleases, exonucleases, ligases, polymerases) and match each one against the function in its option to find the pair that is correct
whyOne direct recall of enzyme functions that the chapter's 'Tools of Recombinant DNA Technology' section states, checked against four one-line pairings with nothing to calculate or infer.

(a)Floating Ribs(i)Located between second & seventh ribs
(b)Acromion(ii)Head of the Humerus
(c)Scapula(iii)Clavicle
(d)Glenoid cavity(iv)Do not connect with the sternum
  1. (a) - (iii); (b) - (ii); (c) - (iv); (d) - (i)
  2. (a) - (iv); (b) - (iii); (c) - (i); (d) - (ii)

Identify the wrong statement with regard to Restriction Enzymes.
  1. They are useful in genetic engineering
  2. Sticky ends can be joined by using DNA ligases
  3. Each restriction enzyme functions by inspecting the length of a DNA sequence
  4. They cut the strand of DNA at palindromic sites
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · medium
  1. Option 1: restriction enzymes are the molecular scissors of genetic engineering, so it is true.
  2. Option 2: sticky ends formed by restriction enzymes are joined by DNA ligase, so it is true.
  3. Option 3: NCERT states each restriction endonuclease functions by inspecting the length of a DNA sequence, so it is true.

the catchThe statements closely paraphrase NCERT wording, so the candidate has to remember the exact phrasing about how the enzyme scans DNA and where it cuts relative to the palindrome; a statement that sounds odd may be verbatim NCERT, and one that sounds right may be loosely worded.

(a)Zygotene(i)Terminalization
(b)Pachytene(ii)Chiasmata

(d)Diakinesis(iv)Synapsis
  1. a-(i), b-(ii), c-(iv), d-(iii)
  2. a-(ii), b-(iv), c-(iii), d-(i)

  1. Zygotene is when homologous chromosomes pair up, i.e. synapsis: a → (iv).
  2. Pachytene is when crossing over occurs between non-sister chromatids: b → (iii).

  4. Diakinesis is marked by terminalization of chiasmata: d → (i).
  5. The match a-(iv), b-(iii), c-(ii), d-(i) is option 4.

Q38Biology / zoologyno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterExcretory Products and Their Elimination high confidence
why this chapterEvery option turns on hormonal regulation of kidney function (aldosterone-driven Na+ and water reabsorption from renal tubules, renin, ADH, ANF), which is taught in Excretory Products and Their Elimination.
subtopic 1Regulation Of Kidney Function
why this subtopicEvery option, including the aldosterone-driven Na+ and water reabsorption that prevents diuresis, turns on the hormonal control of kidney function (ADH, renin-angiotensin-aldosterone, ANF).
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Which of the following would help in prevention of diuresis?
  1. Atrial natriuretic factor causes vasoconstriction
  2. Decrease in secretion of renin by JG cells
  3. More water reabsorption due to undersecretion of ADH
  4. Reabsorption of Na+ and water and from renal tubules due to aldosterone
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. Preventing diuresis means the body must conserve water, so less urine is formed.
  2. Option 1 is false: ANF causes vasodilation and opposes the RAA mechanism, which promotes diuresis.
  3. Option 2: less renin from JG cells ⇒ less angiotensin II and aldosterone ⇒ less Na+ and water reabsorption, so diuresis increases.
  4. Option 3 contradicts itself: undersecretion of ADH lowers water reabsorption, which causes diuresis.
  5. Option 4: aldosterone increases reabsorption of Na+ and water from the renal tubules, so less urine forms and diuresis is prevented. The answer is option 4.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—4 highunchecked
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsRecall that diuresis means increased urine output, so its prevention requires mechanisms that increase Na+/water reabsorption → Recall the regulatory actions of ANF (vasodilator, opposes RAAS), renin/RAAS, ADH and aldosterone on renal tubules → Apply each mechanism's direction of effect to every option, rejecting those that are factually wrong (ANF as vasoconstrictor, ADH undersecretion causing more reabsorption) or that increase urine output (reduced renin)
whyThe candidate must recall four hormonal controls of kidney function and then judge, option by option, whether each stated mechanism is correct and whether it reduces or increases urine output.
the catchTwo options pair a real hormone with a wrong action or an internally contradictory cause-effect (ANF 'vasoconstriction'; 'more water reabsorption due to undersecretion of ADH'), so a candidate reading only the outcome clause can be misled.

subtopic 1Mendelian Disorders
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the mode of inheritance (autosomal recessive/dominant, X-linked, Y-linked) of four Mendelian disorders - sickle-cell anaemia, thalassemia, haemophilia and phenylketonuria - which are taught together under Mendelian Disorders.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Select the correct match.
  1. Sickle cell anaemia - Autosomal recessive trait, chromosome-11
  2. Thalassemia - X linked
  3. Haemophilia - Y linked
  4. Phenylketonuria - Autosomal dominant traint
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. Sickle cell anaemia is an autosomal recessive disorder; the β-globin gene that carries the mutation (GAG → GUG, Glu → Val at position 6) lies on chromosome 11, so option 1 is correct.
  2. Thalassemia is an autosomal recessive blood disorder (α on chromosome 16, β on chromosome 11), not X-linked, so option 2 is wrong.
  3. Haemophilia is a sex-linked recessive disease carried on the X chromosome, not Y-linked, so option 3 is wrong.
  4. Phenylketonuria is an autosomal recessive inborn error of metabolism, not autosomal dominant, so option 4 is wrong; the answer is option 1.

Q77Biology / zoologyno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchzoology
chapterHuman Reproduction high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the hormonal control of ovulation during the menstrual cycle (high estrogen triggering the mid-cycle LH surge that releases the ovum from the Graafian follicle), which is taught in Human Reproduction.
subtopic 1Menstrual Cycle
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the hormone peak (high estrogen inducing the mid-cycle LH surge) that triggers ovulation, which is taught under the menstrual cycle.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Which of the following hormone levels will cause release of ovum (ovulation) from the graafian follicle?
  1. Low concentration of LH
  2. Low concentration of FSH
  3. High concentration of Estrogen
  4. High concentration of progesterone
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · medium
  1. In the follicular phase the growing Graafian follicle secretes more and more estrogen, which reaches a peak just before mid-cycle.
  2. The high estrogen level starts the mid-cycle surge of LH (and FSH), and the LH surge causes the Graafian follicle to rupture and release the ovum.
  3. Low LH or low FSH (options 1 and 2) is the opposite of what happens, and high progesterone (option 4) comes from the corpus luteum after ovulation.
  4. This leaves high concentration of estrogen, which is option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—3 mediumunchecked
difficultymedium · 2 steps
stepsRecall from the menstrual-cycle account that ovulation is triggered by a mid-cycle LH surge, and note that the only LH option states a LOW concentration, so it cannot be chosen directly → Link back one step in the hormonal chain: identify which listed hormone level, near the middle of the cycle, brings about the LH surge (and so ovulation), and rule out the remaining options as reversed or belonging to the luteal phase
whyThe direct recall (an LH surge causes ovulation) is not among the options, so the candidate must follow the hormonal chain one step further back to the cause of that surge and eliminate the inverted LH/FSH options and the luteal-phase hormone.
the catchThe option naming LH, the hormone a candidate associates with ovulation, is stated as 'low', which catches a candidate who matches on the hormone's name without reading the level; the actual cause of the surge is a positive-feedback link that NCERT mentions only briefly.

Q80Biology / botanyno printed answer found
subjectBiology
branchbotany
chapterSexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants high confidence
why this chapterEvery statement and option depends on the gametophyte-within-sporophyte relationship of pollen grains in the anther, the embryo sac in the ovule and the seed in the fruit, which Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants teaches.
subtopic 1Alternation of Generations
why this subtopicThe answer depends on recognising a gametophyte generation (pollen grain, embryo sac) enclosed within sporophyte tissue (anther, ovule), which is the sporophyte-gametophyte relationship of alternation of generations.
printed in the booklet
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The plant parts which consist of two generations one within the other:
(a)Pollen grains inside the anther
(b)Germinated pollen grain with two male gametes
(c)Seed inside the fruit
(d)Embryo sac inside the ovule
  1. (c) and (d)
  2. (a) and (d)
  3. (a) only
  4. (a), (b) and (c)
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

no worked solution is printed for this question

Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. (a) Pollen grains are male gametophytes (haploid, n) held inside the anther, which is sporophytic tissue (2n): two generations, one within the other.
  2. (b) A germinated pollen grain with its two male gametes is all male gametophyte (n): one generation only.
  3. (c) A seed (embryo, seed coat) inside a fruit (pericarp) is sporophytic tissue within sporophytic tissue: not gametophyte within sporophyte.
  4. (d) The embryo sac is the female gametophyte (n) inside the ovule, which is sporophytic (2n): two generations, one within the other.
  5. So (a) and (d) are correct, which is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—2 highunchecked
difficultymedium · 3 steps

  2. Zero

Q92Physicsno printed answer found
subjectPhysics
chapterAlternating Current high confidence
why this chapterThe answer depends on the series LCR circuit relation tan(phi) = (XL - XC)/R: equal phase differences show XL = XC, so the circuit is at resonance and its power factor is 1.
subtopic 1Power in an AC Circuit
subtopic 2Resonance
why this subtopicEqual phase angles with L removed and with C removed give X_L = X_C, so the LCR circuit is at resonance and its power factor cos(phi) is 1.
printed in the booklet
publisher’s solution, as printed
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
A series LCR circuit is connected to an ac voltage source. When L is removed from the circuit, the phase difference between current and voltage Is π/3. If instead C is removed from the circuit, the phase difference is again π/3 between current and voltage. The power factor of the circuit is
  1. 1.0
  2. -1.0
  3. Zero
  4. 0.5
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
  1. tan 60o = Xc12; XL = XC
  2. tan 60o = XL2

→ the working concludes: unclear

printed solution, as in the booklet
Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. With L removed (RC circuit): tanπ3 = XCR, so XC = 3R.
  2. With C removed (RL circuit): tanπ3 = XLR, so XL = 3R.
  3. Hence XL = XC, so for the full LCR circuit Z = R2 + (XL - XC)2 = R.
  4. Power factor cosφ = RZ = 1, which is option 1.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—1 highunchecked
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsWith L removed (RC circuit), write tan(π/3) = X_C/R → With C removed (RL circuit), write tan(π/3) = X_L/R, and equate the two to get X_L = X_C → Recognise X_L = X_C as resonance in the full LCR circuit, so Z = R and power factor cos(φ) = R/Z
whyThe work is writing the phase-angle relation for the RC and RL sub-circuits, equating them to conclude X_L = X_C, then applying the resonance condition to get the power factor of the full circuit.
the catchOne option equals cos(pi/3), the trap for a candidate who takes the given sub-circuit phase angle as the phase of the full LCR circuit.

Q93Physicsno printed answer found
subjectPhysics
chapterModern Physics high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the photoelectric effect's threshold frequency: halving the frequency drops it below threshold, so no photoelectrons are emitted whatever the intensity.
subtopic 1Photoelectric Effect
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the threshold-frequency condition of the photoelectric effect: halving 1.5 times the threshold frequency takes it below threshold, so no photoemission occurs whatever the intensity.
printed in the booklet
publisher’s solution, as printed
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
Light of frequency 1.5 times the threshold frequency W incident on a photosensitive material. What will be the photoelectric current if the frequency is halved and intensity is doubled ?
  1. one – fourth
  2. zero
  3. doubled
  4. four times
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
  1. Theoretical

→ the working concludes: unclear

printed solution, as in the booklet
Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. Initial frequency ν = 1.5 ν0, where ν0 is the threshold frequency.
  2. Halving gives ν' = 1.5 ν02 = 0.75 ν0.
  3. Since ν' < ν0, no photoelectrons are emitted, regardless of intensity.
  4. Doubling the intensity does not change this, so the photoelectric current is zero, which is option 2.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—2 highunchecked
difficultymedium · 2 steps
stepsCompute the new frequency: half of 1.5 times the threshold frequency gives 0.75 times the threshold, so it is below threshold → Recall that below the threshold frequency no photoelectrons are emitted whatever the intensity, so the change in intensity has no effect on the current
whyOne short arithmetic step to compare the new frequency with the threshold, then one recall of the threshold condition for photoemission.
the catchThe 'doubled' option is what a candidate gets by applying current proportional to intensity without first checking the frequency against the threshold.

why this chapterThe answer depends on the sign of the temperature coefficient of resistance, which is taught under Temperature Coefficient of Resistance and Temperature Dependence of Resistivity in Current Electricity.
subtopic 1Temperature Coefficient of Resistance
why this subtopicThe answer depends on which classes of solids have a negative temperature coefficient of resistance, which is taught under the temperature coefficient of resistance.
printed in the booklet
publisher’s solution, as printed
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The solids which have the negative temperature coefficient of resistance are :
  1. Semiconductors only
  2. Insulators and semiconductors
  3. Metals
  4. Insulators only
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
  1. εm = t0.k
  2. = 5 × 0.05 × 10-12
  3. = 44.25 × 10-12
  4. = 0.44 × 10-10

→ the working concludes: unclear

printed solution, as in the booklet
Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. In metals, rising T increases collisions while n stays fixed, so ρ rises and α > 0.
  2. In semiconductors and insulators, rising T excites electrons across the band gap, so n rises sharply and ρ falls, giving α < 0.
  3. Both semiconductors and insulators have negative α, which is option 2.

subtopic 1Transistor Action

printed in the booklet
publisher’s solution, as printed
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
For transistor action, which of the following statements is correct?
  1. Both emitter junction as well as the collector junction are forward biased.
  2. The base region must be very thin and lightly doped.
  3. Base, emitter and collector regions should have same doping concentrations
  4. Base, emitter and collector regions should have same size
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
  1. Semiconductor.

→ the working concludes: unclear

printed solution, as in the booklet
Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
  1. In the active mode the emitter-base junction is forward biased and the collector-base junction is reverse biased, so option 1 is false.
  2. The emitter is heavily doped, the base is lightly doped and the collector is moderately doped, so equal doping (option 3) is false.
  3. The base is very thin and the collector is the largest region, so equal size (option 4) is false.
  4. A very thin, lightly doped base lets most carriers injected from the emitter reach the collector, so option 2 is correct.

publisher’s solution, as printed
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The Brewsters angle ib for an interface should be:
  1. 450 < ib < 900
  2. ib = 900
  3. 00 < ib < 300
  4. 300 < ib < 450
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
  1. μ = tan θ1
  2. 1 < μ < 90o
  3. 45o < θ < 90o

→ the working concludes: option 1

printed solution, as in the booklet
Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · medium
  1. Brewster's law: tan ib = n2n1.
  2. For light incident from a rarer medium on a denser one (the NCERT case, e.g. air to glass), n2n1 > 1, so tan ib > 1 and ib > 45°.
  3. n2n1 is finite, so tan ib is finite and ib < 90°.
  4. Hence 45° < ib < 90°, which is option 1.

That puts i_b between 45 and 90 degrees, which picks the matching range from the options
whyThe work is recalling the Brewster's law relation and then turning the condition that the relative refractive index exceeds 1 into a bound on the tangent, and so on the angle range.
the catchThe stem does not say that light travels from a rarer to a denser medium; the candidate has to assume it to get tan i_b > 1.

subtopic 1Prism
why this subtopicThe answer depends on refraction through a thin prism: emergence is normal so r2 = 0, r1 = A, and with small angles i = mu*r1 = mu*A.
printed in the booklet
publisher’s solution, as printed
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
A ray is incident at an angle of incidence i on one surface of a small angle prism (with angle of prism A) and emerges normally from the opposite surface. If the refractive index of the material of the prism is μ, then the angle of incidence is nearly equal to
  1. μ A
  2. μ A/2
  3. A/2μ
  4. 2A/μ
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
  1. μ = sin 1osin A
  2. i = μ A

→ the working concludes: option 1

printed solution, as in the booklet
Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
  1. The ray emerges normally from the second face, so the angle of refraction there is r2 = 0.
  2. For a prism r1 + r2 = A, so r1 = A.
  3. Snell's law at the first face: sin i = μ sin r1 = μ sin A.
  4. For small angles sin i ≈ i and sin A ≈ A, so i ≈ μ A, which is option 1.

whyThe work is using the prism-geometry relation r1 + r2 = A with r2 = 0, then Snell's law at the entry face, then the small-angle approximation.

chapterThermodynamics medium confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on the kinetic-theory result that a mono-atomic gas molecule has average thermal energy (3/2)k_BT, which is taught under 'Kinetic Theory of an Ideal Gas' in this taxonomy's Thermodynamics chapter.
subtopic 1Kinetic Theory of an Ideal Gas
why this subtopicThe answer depends on the kinetic-theory result that a monatomic gas molecule's average thermal (translational kinetic) energy is (3/2)k_BT, via equipartition over three translational degrees of freedom.
printed in the booklet
publisher’s solution, as printed
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The average thermal energy for a mono-atomic gas is : (kB is Boltzmann constant and T, absolute temperature)
  1. 52kBT
  2. 72kBT
  3. 12kBT
  4. 32kBT
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
  1. U = f2nRT
  2. = f2NNART
  3. = f2N(RNA)T
  4. U = f2NkBT
  5. Where N → No. of atoms
  6. NA → Avogadro Number
  7. U → Total thermal energy
  8. T → Temperature
  9. R → Gas constant
  10. KB → Boltzmann constant
  11. Average energy = U/N
  12. ∴ Average thermal energy = f2kBT
  13. For mono-atomic gas f = 3
  14. Uavg = 32kBT

→ the working concludes: option 4

printed solution, as in the booklet
Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
  1. A monoatomic gas molecule has only translational motion, so f = 3 degrees of freedom.
  2. By the law of equipartition each degree of freedom carries 12kBT.
  3. E = 3 × 12kBT = 32kBT, which is option 4.

READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The ratio of contributions made by the electric field and magnetic field components to the intensity of an electromagnetic wave is : (c = speed of electromagnetic waves)
  1. 1:c
  2. 1:c2
  3. c:1
  4. 1:1
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
  1. E0 = CB0 → E0B0 = C
  2. E0 → Amplitude electric field
  3. C → Speed of light
  4. B → Amplitude of magnetic field
  5. E.M. wave consists of electric and magnetic field.
  6. Intensity x Energy Average energy density due to electric field
  7. dUau|avg = 12ε0 E02
  8. Average energy density due to magnetic field

  1. Electric energy density: uE = 12ε0 E2.
  2. Magnetic energy density: uB = B22μ0.
  3. Using B = Ec and c2 = 1μ0 ε0: uB = E22μ0 c2 = 12ε0 E2 = uE.
  4. Intensity I = (uE + uB)c, so the contributions are in the ratio uE : uB = 1:1, which is option 4.

Q113Physicsno printed answer found
subjectPhysics
chapterThermal Properties of Matter high confidence
why this chapterThe answer turns on heat capacity Q = m s dT with mass proportional to r^3 for the same material, which is specific heat capacity in Thermal Properties of Matter.
subtopic 1Specific Heat Capacity and Its Types
why this subtopicThe answer depends on heat capacity being mass times specific heat (Q = mc for 1 K), so for the same material the ratio equals the mass ratio (r1/r2)^3.
printed in the booklet
publisher’s solution, as printed
READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
The quantities of heat required to raise the temperature of two solid copper spheres of radii r1 and r2 (r1 = 1.5 r2) through 1 K are in the ratio
  1. 3/2
  2. 5/3
  3. 27/8
  4. 9/4
The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
  1. General formula for heat required in order to raise the temperature of an object mass m by Δ T is
  2. Where s is specific heat
  3. Given are to solid sphere of radius r1 and r2
  4. Both spheres are made up of same material, so their density and specific heat capacities will be same
  5. Mass of sphere -1 i.e m1 = p(43π r13)
  6. Mass of sphere -2 i.e m1 = p(43π r23)
  7. Both spheres are raised by same temperature = Δ T
  8. So Heat required Q1 for sphere -1 is
  9. Q1 = m1 sΔ T
  10. So Heat required Q2 for sphere -2 is
  11. Q2 = m2 sΔ T
  12. ∴ Q1Q2 = m1 sΔ Tm2Δ T = m1m2 = ρ(43π e13)ρ(43π r23)
  13. = (r1r2)3 = (32)2 = 278

→ the working concludes: option 3

printed solution, as in the booklet
Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
  1. For the same Δ T = 1 K and the same specific heat s, Q = m s Δ T ∝ m.
  2. m = ρ · 43π r3, so m ∝ r3 for the same material.
  3. Q1Q2 = (r1r2)3 = (32)3 = 278, which is option 3.
printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
—3 highunchecked
difficultymedium · 3 steps
stepsWrite Q = m s dT and note that same material (same s) and same dT make Q proportional to mass → Express mass as density times sphere volume, so m is proportional to r3 → Compute the ratio (r1/r2)3 = (1.5)3 as a fraction
whyThe work is recognising that heat at fixed specific heat and temperature rise scales with mass, chaining mass to the cube of the radius, and cubing 3/2.
the catchDistractors 3/2 and 9/4 are exactly the results of scaling heat with radius or with surface area instead of volume.

    chapterSemiconductor Electronics high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on how biasing a p-n junction diode changes its depletion-region width, which is taught in semiconductor electronics.
    subtopic 1PN Junction Diode Under Reverse Bias
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on the fact that a reverse bias widens the depletion region of a p-n junction, which is taught under the diode in reverse bias.
    printed in the booklet
    publisher’s solution, as printed
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    The increase in the width of the depletion region in a p-n junction diode is due to :
    1. Both forward bias and reverse bias
    2. Increase in forward current
    3. Forward bias only
    4. Reverse bias only
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
    1. Forward Biasing
    2. Width Depletion layer decreases
    3. Reverse Biasing
    4. Width of depletion Layer decreases

    → the working concludes: unclear

    printed solution, as in the booklet
    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
    1. Under forward bias the applied voltage V opposes the built-in potential, so the barrier becomes V0 - V and the depletion region narrows.
    2. Under reverse bias the applied voltage adds to the built-in potential, so the barrier becomes V0 + V and the depletion region widens.
    3. So the depletion width increases only under reverse bias, which is option 4.

    Q120Physicsno printed answer found
    subjectPhysics
    chapterWaves high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on beat frequency as the difference of two string frequencies, plus how a string's frequency changes with tension, both taught in Waves (Modes and Beats).
    subtopic 1Modes and Beats
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on beat frequency being the difference of the two string frequencies and on how that beat count changes when one string's frequency is lowered, which is taught under beats.
    printed in the booklet
    publisher’s solution, as printed
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    In a guitar, two strings A and B made of same material are slightly out of tune and produce beats of frequency 6 Hz. When tension in B is slightly decreased, the beat frequency increases to 7 Hz. If the frequency of A is 530 Hz, the original frequency of B will be:
    1. 536 Hz
    2. 537 Hz
    3. 523 Hz
    4. 524 Hz
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
    1. f = 1LTμ
    2. f α T
    3. f1 - f2 = 6 Hz
    4. 530 - f2 = 6
    5. f2 = 54 Hz
    6. Tension in T is decreased So its frequency will decrease, So that is why the no beats will increase so from here we can say f1 > f2

    → the working concludes: option 4

    printed solution, as in the booklet
    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
    1. Beat frequency 6 Hz gives fB = 530 ± 6, i.e. 524 Hz or 536 Hz.
    2. For a string f ∝ T, so decreasing the tension in B lowers fB.
    3. If fB = 536 Hz, lowering it moves it towards 530 Hz and the beat frequency would fall below 6 Hz, which contradicts the data.
    4. If fB = 524 Hz, lowering it to 523 Hz gives 530 - 523 = 7 Hz beats, as stated.
    5. So the original fB = 524 Hz, which is option 4.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —4 highunchecked
    difficultymedium · 3 steps
    stepsFrom the 6 Hz beat frequency, list the two candidates for B's frequency: 530 + 6 or 530 - 6 Hz → Recall that a string's frequency goes as the square root of its tension, so lowering B's tension lowers B's frequency → Check each candidate: the beat frequency rises to 7 Hz only if B was already below A, so the higher candidate is ruled out
    whyFinding the two values that fit the beat frequency, then using the frequency-tension relation (f proportional to T) to decide which way B moves when its tension drops and so which value is consistent with the beats rising.

    → the working concludes: unclear

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
    1. With L removed (RC circuit): tanπ3 = XCR, so XC = 3R.
    2. With C removed (RL circuit): tanπ3 = XLR, so XL = 3R.
    3. Hence XL = XC, so for the full LCR circuit Z = R2 + (XL - XC)2 = R.
    4. Power factor cosφ = RZ = 1, which is option 1.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —1 highunchecked
    difficultymedium · 3 steps
    stepsWith L removed (RC circuit), write tan(π/3) = X_C / R → With C removed (RL circuit), write tan(π/3) = X_L / R, and equate the two to get X_L = X_C → Recognise X_L = X_C as resonance in the full LCR circuit, so the impedance is R and the power factor is cos(φ) = R/Z
    whyThe work is writing the phase-angle relation for each reduced circuit, equating them to get equal reactances, and recognising that equal reactances mean resonance in the full circuit.
    the catchOption 0.5 is exactly cos(pi/3), the answer a candidate gets by carrying the given phase angle over to the full circuit instead of combining the two reactances.

    subjectPhysics
    chapterModern Physics high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on the photoelectric threshold condition: no photoemission occurs below the threshold frequency regardless of intensity, which is taught under Photoelectric Effect in Modern Physics.
    subtopic 1Photoelectric Effect
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on the threshold-frequency condition of the photoelectric effect: halving the frequency puts it below threshold, so no photocurrent flows whatever the intensity.
    printed in the booklet
    publisher’s solution, as printed
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    Light of frequency 1.5 times the threshold frequency W incident on a photosensitive material. What will be the photoelectric current if the frequency is halved and intensity is doubled ?
    1. one – fourth
    2. zero
    3. doubled
    4. four times
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
    1. Theoretical

    → the working concludes: unclear

    printed solution, as in the booklet
    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
    1. The incident frequency is ν = 1.5 ν0, where ν0 is the threshold frequency.
    2. Halving it gives ν' = 1.5 ν02 = 0.75 ν0.
    3. ν' < ν0, so no photoelectrons are emitted, whatever the intensity.
    4. Doubling the intensity cannot cause emission below threshold, so the photoelectric current is zero, which is option 2.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —2 highunchecked
    difficultymedium · 2 steps
    stepsCompute the new frequency relative to threshold: 1.5 times threshold halved gives 0.75 times threshold, i.e. below threshold → Apply the threshold law of the photoelectric effect: below threshold frequency no photoelectrons are emitted whatever the intensity, so the change in intensity is irrelevant
    whyThe work is one arithmetic comparison of the halved frequency against the threshold followed by applying the NCERT rule that emission needs frequency above threshold independent of intensity.
    the catchThe doubled-intensity clause is a distractor: a candidate who applies 'photocurrent is proportional to intensity' without first checking the threshold lands on the 'doubled' option.

    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader

    2. Insulators and semiconductors

    1. For metals, resistivity rises with temperature, so α > 0; this rules out option 3.

    chapterRay Optics high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on refraction through a thin prism (Snell's law at the first face with r1 = A since the ray emerges normally, giving i ≈ μA), which is taught in Ray Optics.
    subtopic 1Prism
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on refraction through a small-angle prism, where normal emergence makes the internal refraction angle equal to the prism angle A, so i is about muA.
    printed in the booklet
    publisher’s solution, as printed
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    A ray is incident at an angle of incidence i on one surface of a small angle prism (with angle of prism A) and emerges normally from the opposite surface. If the refractive index of the material of the prism is μ, then the angle of incidence is nearly equal to
    1. μ A
    2. μ A/2
    3. A/2 μ
    4. 2A/μ
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)
    1. μ = sin 1osin A
    2. i = μ A

    → the working concludes: option 1

    printed solution, as in the booklet
    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
    1. The ray emerges normally from the second face, so the refraction angle there is r2 = 0.
    2. For a prism r1 + r2 = A, so r1 = A.
    3. Snell's law at the first face: sin i = μ sin r1 = μ sin A.
    4. For small angles sin i ≈ i and sin A ≈ A, so i ≈ μ A, which is option 1.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —1 highunchecked
    difficultymedium · 3 steps

    whyThe work is turning normal emergence into r2 = 0, using r1 + r2 = A to fix the internal refraction angle, then applying small-angle Snell's law at the entry face.
    the catchOne option is exactly what a candidate gets by wrongly assuming minimum deviation (r1 = A/2) instead of normal emergence.

    Q139Chemistry / physicalno printed answer found
    subjectChemistry
    streamphysical
    chapterThermodynamics high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on the signs of q, w and change in temperature for adiabatic free expansion of an ideal gas, which is the first law of thermodynamics and work done in Thermodynamics.
    subtopic 1Internal Energy and First Law of Thermodynamics
    why this subtopicThe answer rests on the first law: free expansion gives w = 0, adiabatic gives q = 0, so ΔU = q + w = 0 and hence ΔT = 0 for an ideal gas.
    printed in the booklet
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    The correct option for free expansion of an ideal gas under adiabatic condition is
    1. q < 0, Δ T = 0 and w = 0
    2. q > 0, Δ T > 0 and w > 0
    3. q = 0, Δ T = 0 and w = 0
    4. q = 0, Δ T < 0 and w > 0
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
    1. Adiabatic condition: no heat exchange, so q = 0.
    2. Free expansion is against pext = 0, so w = -pextΔ V = 0.
    3. Δ U = q + w = 0 + 0 = 0.
    4. For an ideal gas U depends only on T, so Δ U = 0 ⇒ Δ T = 0.
    5. q = 0, Δ T = 0, w = 0 is option 3.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —3 highunchecked
    difficultyeasy · 1 step
    stepsRecall the NCERT statement that free expansion of an ideal gas (into vacuum, adiabatic) has w = 0 because there is no opposing pressure, q = 0 because it is adiabatic, so δ U = 0 and, for an ideal gas, δ T = 0, then pick the option with all three zero
    whyThe work is one recall of the free-expansion case that NCERT's thermodynamics chapter states explicitly (q = 0, w = 0, δ U = 0 for an ideal gas), followed by matching the option that lists those values.

    Q144Chemistry / organicno printed answer found
    subjectChemistry
    streamorganic
    chapterAldehydes, Ketones, and Carboxylic Acids high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on preparing benzaldehyde from toluene by side-chain chlorination to benzal chloride (C6H5CHCl2) and then hydrolysis at 373 K, which the aldehydes chapter teaches under preparation of benzaldehyde.
    subtopic 1Preparation of Benzaldehyde
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on side-chain chlorination of toluene to benzal chloride (C6H5CHCl2), whose hydrolysis at 373 K gives benzaldehyde, a method taught under preparation of benzaldehyde.
    printed in the booklet
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    Identify compound X in the following aqueous of reaction:

    1. SMILES (printed as a picture, so not redrawn) · ClC(Cl)c1ccccc1 C7H6Cl2 name ✓

    2. SMILES (printed as a picture, so not redrawn) · ClC(Cl)(Cl)c1ccccc1 C7H5Cl3 name ✓

    3. SMILES (printed as a picture, so not redrawn) · Clc1ccccc1 C6H5Cl name ✓

    4. SMILES (printed as a picture, so not redrawn) · ClCc1ccccc1 C7H7Cl name ✓

    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
    1. Cl2/hν chlorinates the side chain of toluene by a free-radical route: C6H5CH3 Cl2/hν  C6H5CHCl2 (benzal chloride).
    2. Hydrolysis at 373 K replaces both Cl by OH: C6H5CHCl2 H2O, 373 K  C6H5CH(OH)2.
    3. The gem-diol loses water: C6H5CH(OH)2 → C6H5CHO + H2O, giving benzaldehyde.
    4. So X is C6H5CHCl2, which is option 1 (CCl3 would give benzoic acid, CH2Cl benzyl alcohol).
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —1 highunchecked
    difficultyeasy · 1 step
    stepsRecall the NCERT route to benzaldehyde by side-chain chlorination of toluene (Cl2/hv) followed by hydrolysis at 373 K, and pick the side-chain gem-dichloride as the intermediate X, the one whose hydrolysis gives the aldehyde.
    whyThe work is one recall of a named NCERT preparation of benzaldehyde, matching the aldehyde's oxidation level to the side-chain chloride that gives it on hydrolysis.

    chapterBasic Concepts of Chemistry high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on the mole concept: number of atoms = (mass / atomic or molar mass) x atoms per formula unit x Avogadro's number, compared for 1 g of each substance.
    subtopic 1Mole Concept
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on converting a fixed mass to moles and then to number of atoms using Avogadro's number (atoms = mass/atomic mass x NA), which is the mole concept.
    printed in the booklet
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    Which one of the followings has maximum number of atoms
    1. 1 g of O2(g) [Atomic mass of O = 16]
    2. 1 g of Li(s) [Atomic mass of Li = 7]
    3. 1 g of Ag(s) [Atomic mass of Ag = 108]
    4. 1 g of Mg(s) [Atomic mass of Mg = 24]
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
    1. Number of atoms ∝ moles of atoms = massmolar mass × atoms per formula unit.
    2. O2: 132 × 2 = 116 ≈ 0.0625 mol atoms.
    3. Li: 17 ≈ 0.143 mol atoms.
    4. Ag: 1108 ≈ 0.0093 mol atoms.
    5. Mg: 124 ≈ 0.0417 mol atoms.
    6. Li has the largest value, so 1 g of Li has the most atoms, which is option 2.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict

    whyA single application of the mole relation n = m/M across four equal masses, reducing to picking the lowest atomic mass per atom; a prepared candidate does it mentally.

    Q151Chemistry / physicalno printed answer found
    subjectChemistry
    streamphysical
    chapterStates of Matter high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on Dalton's law of partial pressures (partial pressure = mole fraction x total pressure) for a gas mixture, which is taught in States of Matter.
    subtopic 1Dalton's Law of Partial Pressures
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on Dalton's law: the partial pressure of N2 is its mole fraction times the total pressure.
    printed in the booklet
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    A mixture of N2 and Ar gases in a cylinder contains 7 g of N2 and 8 g of Ar. If the total pressure of the mixture of the gases in the cylinder is 27 bar, the partial pressure of N2 is:
    1. 15 bar
    2. 18 bar
    3. 9 bar
    4. 12 bar
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
    1. nN2 = 728 = 0.25 mol.
    2. nAr = 840 = 0.20 mol.
    3. xN2 = 0.250.25 + 0.20 = 59.
    4. pN2 = xN2   ptotal = 59 × 27 bar = 15 bar, which is option 1.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —1 highunchecked
    difficultymedium · 3 steps
    stepsconvert the given masses to moles using molar masses (N2 = 28, Ar = 40) → form the mole fraction of N2 from its moles over total moles → apply Dalton's law: multiply the mole fraction of N2 by the 27 bar total pressure

    why this subtopicThe incorrect statement is B, and spotting it depends on chromium having the same +6 oxidation state in both CrO4^2- and Cr2O7^2-, as the worked solution in the crop shows.

    CHl was passed through a solution of CaCl2 and NaCl. Which of the following compound(s) crystallise(s)?
    1. Only MgCl2
    2. NaCl, MgCl2 and CaCl2
    3. Both CgCl2 and CaCl2
    4. Only NaCl
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
    1. Passing HCl gas adds Cl- ions: HCl → H+ + Cl-, a common ion for all three chlorides.
    2. Crystallisation needs the ionic product to exceed Ksp; for NaCl, [Na+][Cl-] > Ksp once [Cl-] rises, so pure NaCl crystallises.
    3. CaCl2 and MgCl2 are far more soluble (deliquescent), so they stay in solution; the answer is only NaCl, option 4.

    Q168Chemistry / physicalno printed answer found
    subjectChemistry
    streamphysical
    chapterChemical Kinetics high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on collision theory: raising reactant concentration raises collision frequency, while threshold and activation energy stay unchanged, which is taught in chemical kinetics.
    subtopic 1Collision Theory of Chemical Reactions
    why this subtopicThe answer (collision frequency) depends on the collision-theory fact that more reactant particles per unit volume raise the number of collisions per unit time, while threshold/activation energy and heat of reaction stay unchanged.
    printed in the booklet
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    An increase in the concentration of the reactants of a reaction leads to change in:
    1. Threshold energy
    2. Collision frequency
    3. Activation energy
    4. Heat of reaction
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
    1. By collision theory, rate = ZAB  e-Ea/RT, and the collision frequency ZAB depends on how many reactant molecules are in unit volume.
    2. Raising the concentration raises the number of molecules in unit volume, so ZAB increases.
    3. Threshold energy, Ea and Δ H depend on the nature of the reaction (and on a catalyst), not on concentration, so they do not change. The answer is option 2.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —2 highunchecked
    difficultyeasy · 1 step
    stepsRecall from collision theory (NCERT Chemical Kinetics) which of the listed quantities depend on reactant concentration and which are fixed properties of the reaction (energy barrier, threshold, enthalpy change)
    whyA single recall of collision theory: concentration changes how many molecules are present per unit volume, while the energy terms are intrinsic to the reaction and independent of concentration.

    subjectChemistry
    streaminorganic
    chapterThe D- and F-Block Elements – Practice high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on the 3d4 configuration of the free Cr2+ transition-metal ion (4 unpaired electrons) and the spin-only formula sqrt(n(n+2)), which is taught under magnetic properties of d-block elements.
    subtopic 1Magnetic Properties
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on the spin-only magnetic moment formula applied to the unpaired d-electrons of Cr2+ (d4, 4 unpaired, about 4.90 BM), which is taught under magnetic properties of transition metal ions.
    printed in the booklet
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    The calculated spin only magnetic moment of Cr2+ ion is:
    1. 5.92 BM
    2. 2.84 BM
    3. 3.87 BM
    4. 4.90 BM
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 4 · high
    1. Cr (Z=24) is [Ar] 3d5 4s1, so Cr2+ is [Ar] 3d4.
    2. A 3d4 ion has n = 4 unpaired electrons.
    3. μ = n(n+2) = 4 × 6 = 24 BM.
    4. 24 ≈ 4.90 BM, which is option 4.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict

    whyThe work is a recalled exceptional electron configuration, an ionisation step to count unpaired electrons, and one substitution into the spin-only formula.
    the catchDistractors match plausible slips: writing Cr as 3d4 4s2 and removing 3d electrons gives 2 unpaired (2.84 BM), and stopping at one electron removed (d5) gives 5.92 BM.

    Q171Chemistry / physicalno printed answer found
    subjectChemistry
    streamphysical
    chapterSolutions high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer turns on which binary liquid mixture is a non-ideal solution with positive deviation from Raoult's law, which is taught under ideal and non-ideal solutions in Solutions.
    subtopic 1Ideal and Nonideal Solutions
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on classifying binary mixtures by their positive or negative deviation from Raoult's law (ethanol + acetone shows positive deviation), which is taught under ideal and non-ideal solutions.
    printed in the booklet
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    The mixture which shows positive deviation from Raoult’s law is:
    1. Acetone + Chloroform
    2. Chloroethane + Bromoethane
    3. Ethanol + Acetone
    4. Benzene + Toluene
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 3 · high
    1. Positive deviation from Raoult's law occurs when A-B interactions are weaker than A-A and B-B interactions, so ΔmixH > 0 and p > pRaoult.
    2. Acetone + chloroform forms a hydrogen bond between CHCl3 and the carbonyl oxygen, so it shows negative deviation (option 1 ruled out).
    3. Chloroethane + bromoethane and benzene + toluene are similar molecules and form nearly ideal solutions (options 2 and 4 ruled out).
    4. In ethanol + acetone, acetone molecules come between ethanol molecules and break their hydrogen bonds, weakening interactions, so the mixture shows positive deviation, which is option 3.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —3 highunchecked

    stepsRecall the NCERT Solutions-chapter classification of binary mixtures into ideal, positive-deviation and negative-deviation examples, and pick the pair listed as showing positive deviation
    whyThe four pairs are the standard textbook examples of an ideal solution, a negative deviation and a positive deviation, so the question is a single recall of that classification.

    The freezing point depression constant (Kf) af benzene is 5.12 K kg mol-1. The freezing points depression for the solution of molality 0.087 n containing a non-electrolyte solute in benzene is (rounded off upto two decimal places):

    subtopic 1Thermodynamic Principles of Metallurgy
    why this subtopicThe correct statement (option 2, pig iron can be moulded into a variety of shapes) is about the iron products of blast-furnace extraction of iron, which NCERT teaches under the thermodynamic principles of metallurgy (extraction of iron from its oxides).

    An alkene on ozonolysis gives methanal as one of the product. Its structure is:

    1. SMILES (printed as a picture, so not redrawn) · C=CCC1CCCCC1 C9H16 name ✓

    2. SMILES (printed as a picture, so not redrawn) · CCCC1CCCCC1 C9H18 name ✓

    3. SMILES (printed as a picture, so not redrawn) · CC=CC1CCCCC1 C9H16 name ✓

    4. SMILES (printed as a picture, so not redrawn) · CCCC1C=CCCC1 C9H16 name ✓

    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 1 · high
    1. Ozonolysis cleaves C=C into two carbonyls; methanal HCHO forms only from a terminal =CH2 carbon.
    2. Option 1: C6H11-CH2-CH=CH2 O3, Zn/H2O  C6H11-CH2-CHO + HCHO, so methanal forms.
    3. Option 2 (C6H11CH2CH2CH3) is an alkane with no C=C, so no ozonolysis.
    4. Option 3 (C6H11-CH=CH-CH3) gives C6H11CHO + CH3CHO, no methanal.
    5. Option 4 (ring C=C in 3-propylcyclohexene) gives a single open-chain dialdehyde, no methanal.
    6. Only option 1 gives methanal.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —1 highunchecked
    difficultyeasy · 1 step
    stepsRecall that ozonolysis gives methanal (HCHO) only from a terminal =CH2 carbon of a C=C bond, and pick the one option structure that has a terminal C=CH2 (the others are saturated, internal or ring alkenes).
    whyThe work is one recall, that a terminal =CH2 cleaves to HCHO, then reading the four drawn structures for a terminal double bond.

    Q180Chemistry / inorganicno printed answer found
    subjectChemistry
    streaminorganic
    chapterChemical Bonding and Molecular Structure high confidence
    why this chapterThe answer depends on whether bond dipoles cancel by molecular symmetry (BF3, BeF2, CO2, 1,4-dichlorobenzene), which is the dipole moment topic of chemical bonding and molecular structure.
    subtopic 1Dipole Moment
    why this subtopicThe answer depends on which molecules have bond dipoles that cancel by symmetry to give zero net dipole moment.
    printed in the booklet
    READ FROM THE IMAGE by b3-question-reader
    Which of the following set of molecules will have zero dipole moment?
    1. Nitrogen trifluoride, beryllium difluoride, water, 1, 3-dichlorobenzene
    2. Boron trifluoride, beryllium difluoride, carbon dioxide, 1, 4-dichlorobenzene
    3. Ammonia, beryllium difluoride, water, 1, 4-dichlorobenzene
    4. Boron trifluoride, hydrogen fluoride, carbon dioxide, 1, 3-dichlorobenzene
    The publisher's solutionprinted answer (none)

    no worked solution is printed for this question

    Solved independently (answer withheld)AI: 2 · high
    1. BF3 is trigonal planar (sp2), so the three B-F bond dipoles at 120° cancel: μ = 0.
    2. BeF2 and CO2 are linear, so their two equal bond dipoles point opposite and cancel: μ = 0.
    3. In 1,4-dichlorobenzene the two C-Cl dipoles are at 180° and cancel: μ = 0.
    4. NF3, NH3 (pyramidal), H2O (bent), HF and 1,3-dichlorobenzene (120° between dipoles) all have μ ≠ 0, ruling out options 1, 3 and 4.
    5. Only option 2 has all four molecules with μ = 0.
    printed in the bookletsolved independentlyverdict
    —2 highunchecked
    difficultymedium · 3 steps
    stepsRecall or derive the shape of each small molecule listed (NF3/NH3 pyramidal, H2O bent, HF diatomic, BeF2 and CO2 linear, BF3 trigonal planar) → Apply bond-dipole vector cancellation to the substituted benzenes: para-dichloro dipoles cancel, meta-dichloro dipoles do not → Eliminate each option that contains at least one polar molecule and keep the set in which every member has zero net dipole
    whyThe work is recalling the geometry of about eight molecules and applying dipole-vector cancellation to each, including the meta/para isomer distinction on the benzene ring, then eliminating options by finding the polar member.
    page 100 · printed answer none · open source PDF at this page · NEET_2020_Code_F1_official.pdf
